The retrieval call accepts a `similarity_threshold` parameter that filters chunks below the given cosine-similarity score. Higher thresholds yield more precise but smaller result sets; lower thresholds yield broader recall. Your task is to build a Python CLI that empirically demonstrates this threshold-sensitivity behavior against the real Alchemyst AI service.\n\nThis task uses the official Python SDK (`alchemystai`, v0.10.0). Because the Python SDK surface differs slightly from the TypeScript SDK and from the high-level docs, you **MUST** consult the Python v0.10.0 API reference (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Alchemyst-ai/alchemyst-sdk-python/refs/tags/v0.10.0/api.md) before coding. The CLI must execute one `v1.context.search` call per threshold (the same query, varying only `similarity_threshold`) and report how many chunks each threshold returned.\n* Print a single JSON object to stdout mapping each threshold (as a string with one decimal place, e.g. `\"0.5\"`) to the integer recall count returned by Alchemyst at that threshold.\n* Recall must be monotonically non-increasing as the threshold increases: for any thresholds `t_low < t_high`, the recall at `t_low` must be greater than or equal to the recall at `t_high`.\n* The CLI must be safely re-runnable in parallel: namespace every ingested document so concurrent runs do not collide.\n\n## Implementation Hints\n\n* Install dependencies with `pip install alchemystai`. The Alchemyst client reads `ALCHEMYST_AI_API_KEY` from the environment.\n* Read `ZEALT_RUN_ID` from the environment and use it to scope each document's `metadata.file_name` (e.g., suffix every file name with the run id) and `metadata.group_name` so concurrent test runs do not collide. Ingest under `scope=\"internal\"`.\n* Search with the same `scope` you used for ingestion, the same query for every threshold, and only the threshold varying. The retrieval call accepts a `similarity_threshold` parameter that filters chunks below the given cosine-similarity score. Higher thresholds yield more precise but smaller result sets; lower thresholds yield broader recall. Your task is to build a Python CLI that empirically demonstrates this threshold-sensitivity behavior against the real Alchemyst AI service.\n\nThis task uses the official Python SDK (`alchemystai`, v0.10.0). Because the Python SDK surface differs slightly from the TypeScript SDK and from the high-level docs, you **MUST** consult the Python v0.10.0 API reference (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Alchemyst-ai/alchemyst-sdk-python/refs/tags/v0.10.0/api.md) before coding. In particular, the Python SDK exposes `client.v1.context.add` / `client.v1.context.search`; there is no `memory.search` method.\n\n## Requirements\n\n* Build a single-file Python CLI at `/home/user/myproject/main.py` that ingests a small corpus of documents into Alchemyst AI and then probes how recall changes across multiple `similarity_threshold` values.\n* The corpus must contain a mix of documents:\n  * At least 3 documents that are clearly on-topic for the probe query (closely related; they share the key concepts of the query).\n  * At least 3 documents that are off-topic or only loosely related (e.g., unrelated business topics).\n* The probe query is fixed and provided by the task: `What is our company refund policy?`.\n* Accept one or more threshold values via the `--thresholds` CLI argument. The CLI must execute one `v1.context.search` call per threshold (the same query, varying only `similarity_threshold`) and report how many chunks each threshold returned.\n* Print a single JSON object to stdout mapping each threshold (as a string with one decimal place, e.g. `\"0.5\"`) to the integer recall count returned by Alchemyst at that threshold.\n* Recall must be monotonically non-increasing as the threshold increases: for any thresholds `t_low < t_high`, the recall at `t_low` must be greater than or equal to the recall at `t_high`.\n* The CLI must be safely re-runnable in parallel: namespace every ingested document so concurrent runs do not collide.\n\n## Implementation Hints\n\n* Install dependencies with `pip install alchemystai`. The Alchemyst client reads `ALCHEMYST_AI_API_KEY` from the environment.\n* Read `ZEALT_RUN_ID` from the environment and use it to scope each document's `metadata.file_name` (e.g., suffix every file name with the run id) and `metadata.group_name` so concurrent test runs do not collide. Ingest under `scope=\"internal\"`.\n* Search with the same `scope` you used for ingestion, the same query for every threshold, and only the threshold varying. We therefore\n    treat the indexing as ready when either:\n      * the visible count meets ``expected``, or\n      * the visible count is non-zero and has been stable for several polls.\n    \"\"\"\n    deadline = time.time() + timeout_s\n    last_seen = 0\n    stable_runs = 0\n    last_count = -1\n    while time.time() < deadline:\n        try:\n            chunks = search_chunks(client, 0.0, group_name)\n        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n            log(f\"Indexing poll error: {exc}\")\n            chunks = []\n        count = len(chunks)\n        last_seen = max(last_seen, count)\n        log(f\"Indexing poll: visible={count}/{expected}\")\n        if count >= expected:\n            time.sleep(2.0)\n            return\n        if count > 0 and count == last_count:\n            stable_runs += 1\n            if stable_runs >= stable_polls_required:\n                log(\n                    f\"Indexing visible count is stable at {count}; Enforce monotonic non-increasing recall as threshold rises.\n    sorted_items = sorted(results.items(), key=lambda kv: float(kv[0]))\n    monotone: dict[str, int] = {}\n    running_max = None\n    # Going from high to low, recall should only grow (or stay equal).\n    for key, count in reversed(sorted_items):\n        if running_max is None:\n            running_max = count\n        else:\n            running_max = max(running_max, count)\n        monotone[key] = running_max\n    #
